ASURYANIADVANCEDREACTION
ThisAdvancedReactionisavailableonlytounitsinanAsuryaniDetachment.UnlikeCoreReactions,itisactivatedinuniqueandspecific
circumstances,asnotedinitsdescription,andcanoftenhavegamechangingeffects.AdvancedReactionsuseuppointsofaReactiveplayer’s
ReactionAllotmentasnormalandobeyallotherrestrictionsplaceduponReactions,unlessitisspecificallynotedotherwiseintheirdescription.
AspectsofWar
ThisAdvancedReactionmaybemadeonceperbattleduringanyphasewhenanyenemyunitfulfilsoneofthefollowingconditions:
● Ends a move within 12" and Line of Sight of a unit which is part of an Asuryani Detachment.
● Makes a Shooting Attack targeting a unit which is part of an Asuryani Detachment.
● Declares a Charge targeting a unit which is part of an Asuryani Detachment.
TheReactivePlayermayimmediatelychoosetoperformoneofthefollowingactions:
● The Reacting unit may move a number of inches up to its unmodified Initiative Characteristic in any direction the Reactive Player chooses. In a unit with 
mixed Initiative Characteristics, use the highest unmodified Characteristic. Vehicles may pivot once up to 180° and then move up to 8" directly forwards. 
● The Reacting unit may make a Shooting Attack which is resolved immediately, targeting the unit that triggered this Reaction and following all the usual 
rules for Shooting Attacks. A unit that makes a Shooting Attack as part of this Reaction may not make any attacks indirectly (without line of sight) 
including Barrage weapons or other weapons or Special Rules that otherwise ignore line of sight, and Vehicles may only fire Defensive weapons. Template 
weapons may only be used as part of this Reaction if the target unit is within 8" and must use the Wall of Death rule instead of firing normally. Units 
making a Shooting Attack as part of this Reaction are considered to be Stationary, and may fire weapons of any type as though they had not moved.
● The Reacting unit may count any charges made against it this turn as Disordered.

ASURYANIWARLORDTRAITS
The Asuryani were not a military force at heart, for unlike their Anrathe kin, the Asuryani were a mix of artisans, poets, craftsmen, and more - the average 
citizens of an empire in decline. This was not to say, however, that among their number were not warriors - and as the Path of the Aspects took ahold, many 
among the Asuryani found themselves proficient in the ways of war…
IfyourWarlordhastheAsuryaniSub-type,insteadofchoosingaWarlordTraitfromtheCoreRulebook’sCommonTraits,youmayinsteadchoosea 
WarlordTraitfromtheoptionsbelow.
ASURYANIWARLORDTRAIT:FALCON’SSWIFTNESS
This warrior, and those who travel in his wake, move across broken ruins and blasted battlefields with astonishing certainty and dexterity. 
ThisWarlord,andanyunittheyhavejoined,increasethevalueoftheFleet(X)SpecialRuleby+1,iftheyalreadypossessit,IftheWarlord,oranyunit 
theyhavejoined,doesnothavetheFleet(X)SpecialRule,theygaintheygaintheFleet(1)SpecialRuleinstead.Inaddition,theWarlordgainsthe
PathfinderSpecialRule.
Inaddition,anarmywhoseWarlordhasthisTraitmaymakeanadditionalReactionduringtheOpposingPlayer’sMovementphasesolongasthe
Warlordhasnotbeenremovedasacasualty.
ASURYANIWARLORDTRAIT:AMBUSHOF BLADES
The uncanny senses of this warrior pierce the fog of war. Their tranquillity in combat allows them to predict the precise time at which to strike with his kin, 
commanding a storm of blades and blasts to tear apart the foe. 
Onceperbattle,theControllingPlayerofaWarlordofthisTraitmaydeclaretheuseofthisTraitatthestartofanyphase.Forthedurationofthat
phaseonly,theWarlordandanyunittheyhavejoinedgainthePreferredEnemy(Everything!)SpecialRule.
Inaddition,anarmywhoseWarlordhasthisTraitmaymakeanadditionalReactionduringtheopposingplayer’sAssaultphasesolongasthe
Warlordhasnotbeenremovedasacasualty.
ASURYANIWARLORDTRAIT:SEEROFTHESHIFTINGVECTOR
Wise enough to read the skeins of destiny, whether at their own prowess or with the counsel of others, this warrior sees the many possible futures amidst the 
mayhem of the oncoming battle, allowing them to better coordinate the forces at their disposal.
WhileaWarlordwiththisTraitisinreserves,ordeployedonthebattlefield,theControllingPlayermaychoosetore-rollfailedReservesrolls.
Inaddition,anarmywhoseWarlordhasthisTraitmaymakeanadditionalReactionduringtheopposingplayer’sShootingphasesolongasthe
Warlordhasnotbeenremovedasacasualty.

ASPECTSHRINES
The Aspect Shrines were a relatively new concept on the Craftworlds of the Asuryani during the time coinciding with the Great Crusade; though despite this, 
the Shrines spread across the Craftworlds with surprising speed, the success of their doctrines allowing the nascent Asuryani to defend both their minds and 
their homes with greater effectiveness.
AnExarchorAutarchmustselectoneofthefollowingupgradesatthecostspecifiedontheirprofileaspartofyourarmylistconstruction.Inreturn, 
theygainalloftheSpecialRules,Wargear,UnitTypes,UnitSub-types,andCharacteristicchangesspecifiedfortheirchosenPath.
EachExarchorAutarchmayonlyselectasingleAspectShrineupgrade,unlesstheyareexplicitlygiventheabilitytoselectadditionalonesfrom
anothersource.IfthisoccursandamodelalreadypossessesaversionoftheAspectShrine(X)SpecialRule-anddoesnothaveaversiontoreplace-
simplyaddtherelevantAspectShrine(X)SpecialRuletoitsunitprofileinstead.
WhilsteachAspectShrineupgradenotestheamountoftimesanExarchfromaDetachmentmayselectit(usuallyonlyone),anAutarchmayselect
anyAspectShrineupgradeanddoesnotcounttowardtheamountoftimesitmaybeselectedbyExarchsinthesameDetachment.
ASPECTSHRINE:DARKREAPER
Embodying death as the inescapable harvester of souls, Dark Reapers were a shrine devoted to annihilation at range. No matter how swift, silent, or hardened 
their enemies, a Dark Reaper would seek them out and destroy them. Tales abound of their legendary accuracy, rumours of missiles that could seek targets 
around corners and inside tunnels or drop ships felled from the sky.
ThisAspectShrineupgrademayonlybeselectedbyasingleExarchinaDetachment.
AnAutarchorExarchwhichselectstheDarkReaperAspectShrineupgradegainstheHeavySub-type.

andFastShotSpecialRules.

alreadyequippedwithit),andgainsaReaperLauncher(withStarswarm,Starshot,andStarhawkMissiles)atnoadditionalpointscost.
Inaddition,anAutarchorExarchwhichselectstheDarkReaperAspectShrineupgrademaychoosetoreplacetheirReaperLauncher(with
Starswarm,Starshot,andStarhawkMissiles)foroneofthefollowingoptionsbelowatthecostsshown:

ASPECTSHRINE:DIREAVENGER
The most common of the newly emerged Aspect Shrines, the Dire Avengers were the children of Asurmen, the eldest and great of the Phoenix Lords, founder of 
the Path of the Warrior. Devoted to the high ideals of implacable defence and unrelenting assault, those who trod in Asurmen’s footsteps were flexible warriors 
who would face any odds with immeasurable courage. 
ThisAspectShrineupgrademaybeselectedbyuptothreeExarchsinaDetachment.

ASPECTSHRINE:FIREDRAGON
Dragons, creatures of fire and death, the embodiment of destruction and wrath unleashed upon the world. Such was the totem chosen by the Fire Dragons 
Aspect Shrine, and to its tenants of destruction they held - all who opposed them purged by the fusion weapons that turned their base matter into the tools of 
their own destruction.

Craftworlds as a whole. Called revenants and daemons by those few who observed them, only those of the Aspect itself knew the truth; that they were a martial 
tradition of flowing force, projecting the precision of powerful laser weaponry with pinpoint accuracy.

ASPECTSHRINE:SWOOPINGHAWK
Ancient tales say that Isha wished to gift her children with the power of flight, but the Phoenix King would not allow it, knowing the mischief those same 
children might perform. Now, at the end of their race, the hand of the Phoenix returned to give that gift to the Eldar, whose Aspect Shrines drilled relentlessly 
in the art of flight, using it to deploy the deadly rays of their las weaponry, and the explosive force of their grenade packs. Swiftly spreading as the appeal of 
flight resonated deeply within the minds of every Eldar, the Swooping Hawks were becoming increasingly common as the fall receded into the past.

Whether through oppression or personal rebellion, many chose to walk the Path of the Outcast shortly after The Fall. Many who walked this path, however,

and peerless camouflage, those that fought under the Hunter’s Moon were able to use the battlefield itself to obscure themselves and move effortlessly around

WORLD-RUNE:THETEARSOFLILEATH
Many of the Craftworlds that departed in the wake of the Fall harboured extremely powerful Psykers, or found themselves with exaggerated amounts of 
Psykers in their population. Those which were blessed with such a curse balanced on the tightrope of dread. Where their increased psychic capability provided 
their Craftworld with Intuition and foresight bordering that of prescience, they knew that skeining the threads of fate with such hubris could draw the 
attention of the daemons that haunt them. Whatever the reason a Craftworld cradled them, many Warlocks, Seers, and other psychic warriors followed their 
armies into battle to lend their psychic powers to their comrades. However, this did not save them from the fear and suspicion using such power uncaringly 
bestowed, and what recourse could have occurred as a result.

WORLD-RUNE:THESHATTEREDCHAINS
The birth of the Eye of Terror was unforgiving to the Craftworlds caught in its Vortex during the rapture of the Aeldari. Many escaped its pull, and many fell 
victim to the Great Rift that was created in the wake of their once great empire. There were, however, few who fell under an estranged limbo, not wholly 
consumed by the terrible rift but still caught in its pull. Whether stuck in its proximity for a period of time, or permanently frozen within its reach, these 
shadowy people received little aid and were treated with open suspicion and hostility, for many believe no Aeldari could remain untouched by the predations 
of Chaos for so long. Despite this, those survivors harboured and harnessed an inner steel, granting great strength to those who survived against all odds.

WORLD-RUNE:THECRUCIBLEOFBLOOD
Many Craftworlds who fled following the Fall of the Aeldari harboured more talented craftspeople than talented warriors. These bonesingers’ skills were 
redirected from once peaceful professions to instead produce only the finest Weapons and Engines of Vaul, upscaling their dwindling military power with 
Grav-Tanks and Walkers of consummate firepower. Warhosts provisioned in such a way fielded War-smiths to bring the talented capabilities of their 
bonesingers to practise their art in the heat of battle and even the leaders of such hosts knew the true capabilities of Vaul’s Engines, and spent time amongst 
their own conclaves to learn the ways of the shaper.

## Page 30

The Eldar Path system 
covered all aspects of life; 
from the path of the life, to 
the path of the warrior, the 
heart scribe, and even the 
bonesinger. Some paths were 
rarer than others, and the 
Autarch was one such. 
Autarchs were experienced 
warriors, having trod the 
paths of both guardian and 
Aspect, before finally treading 
the Path of Command.
Focused on the art of strategy 
and logistics, an Autarch was 
capable of crafting 
campaigns, leading from the 
front or from orbit with equal 
precision. Availed of the 
psychic technologies of their 
people an Autarch was able to 
take in the data of thousands 
of warriors when needed and 
issue commands to fights vast 
distances away.
These leaders were greatly 
needed to lead Eldar 
warhosts. With the fall recent 
in the minds of all Asuryani, 
and the hunger of She Who 
Thirsts lurking in the warp, 
the flawless victories and 
clever strategies crafted by the 
Autarchs were pivotal to 
preserving those who 
remained. When combined 
with the peerless foresight of 
the Farseers, Autarchs 
provided the means to give 
Craftworlds the success they 
needed to survive the brutality 
of the Age of Darkness.

The Path system of the 
Craftworlds focused and 
shielded the mind of an Eldar 
from its own vast potential to 
harm itself. This focus was 
levels beyond what ordinary 
humans could achieve, 
encompassing every aspect of 
the walker's life; yet that in 
and of itself became a trap for 
many minds. To the horror of 
early Craftworlders, the Path 
of the Warrior could be trod 
so far and so deeply that the 
walker could not find their 
way off the path to pursue 
any other endeavour. 
Such Eldar became known as 
Exarchs. Living entirely 
within their temples these 
warriors trained to a peerless 
height and both crafted and 
wielded powerful weapons of 
artisanal quality. These same 
Exarchs also trained new 
warriors, and even founded 
new Shrines upon new worlds. 
Through their tireless and 
focused efforts the Aspect 
Shrines spread from 
Craftworld to Craftworld and 
strengthened the forces of the 
recovering Asuryani.

## Page 32

Of all the paths that the 
Asuryani walked, the Path of 
the Seer was perhaps the most 
respected. Eldar technology 
was based upon their innate 
psychic potential, with even 
the smallest constructions 
using psychic controls or 
being powered by psychic 
forces. Yet, much like the 
Aspect Exarchs, this same 
path-based life that protected 
their minds and focused their 
abilities trapped some in a 
singular way of life. For many 
upon the path of the Seer, this 
lead them down to the way of 
the Farseer, powerful diviners 
capable of travelling the skein 
of fate both forward and 
back. 
Such journeys were often 
wrought with danger. 
Thousands of possibilities 
could bubble up from even the 
most innocuous of acts, and 
only the most powerful minds 
could hope to contain this 
information and divine the 
important junctions from the 
unending flow of time itself. 
Farseers used runes, powerful 
symbols that assisted them in 
unlocking parts of their mind 
usually sealed unconsciously 
as to control their mental 
voyage upon the skein. This 
made farseers perhaps the 
most powerful diviners of 
their time, capable of 
foreseeing the outcomes of 
actions centuries before into 
the future thousands of years 
hence.

In the aftermath of the fall, 
the ability of the Anrathe to 
craft and maintain the Ghost 
Warrior automatons was lost; 
their cold wraithbone shells 
falling into disuse and 
disrepair. As the Craftworlds 
began to realise the shape of 
their doom, however, and 
Spirit Stone technology rose 
to prevalence, so too did a 
new class of Aeldari, and a 
new path - the Wraith Guides, 
and the Path of the Dead.
Seen as necromancers by 
many Eldar, and reviled by 
some, the Wraith Guides held 
their duty to safeguard the 
lives and souls of their fellow 
Aeldari as a sacred task. It 
was into this duty that the 
first Wraith Guides entombed 
the Spirit Stones of their 
Craftworld kin into the husks 
of the ancient Ghost Warriors, 
replacing their automata 
minds with that of once-living 
Eldar. 
In times of direst need, the 
Wraith Guides took upon 
themselves the task of guiding 
these new warriors into battle, 
the souls of the once-dead 
denied rest at the behest of an 
age of battle, and of darkness.

## Page 34

The Aeldari known as Eldrad 
Ulthran was still relatively 
young - for one of his long-
lived kind - when the nascent 
Imperium set out across the 
stars to begin the Great 
Crusade. A talented Seer even 
at his young age, he watched 
with eyes both envious and 
sad; Eldrad knew the empire 
humanity set forth to build 
would never again be within 
the purview of the Eldar.
This envy and sadness soured 
to fear within a dangerously 
short time.
Stabbing pain precluded 
visions of treachery, of brother 
turned upon brother, and a 
second race fallen prey to the 
powers of the beyond. 
Desperate to prevent this 
same ancient doom affecting 
another race, he reached to 
contact one of the Primarchs - 
only realising too late that 
Fulgrim, despite his earnest 
outward perception, was in 
fact already tainted... 
Reeling from his discovery, 
Eldrad was forced to watch 
from the sidelines as the 
Imperium turned inward 
upon itself in madness, and 
the events of the fall of his 
race were replayed again in 
another…

## Page 38

Guardians were the mainstay 
of Asyurani armies - militias 
raised from a Craftworld’s 
civilian population. Guardian 
Officers were veterans of 
conflicts before and after the 
Fall, though the innumerable 
dangers the Craftworlds faced 
quickly cultivated many 
battle-hardened troops. 
Inevitably, the responsibility 
of leading Guardian squads 
fell upon these most 
experienced of soldiers.
Whether they led on foot or 
were mounted on a jetbike, 
Guardian Officers were 
afforded more individualised 
equipment both for personal 
protection from would-be 
assassins and to better 
support their squads. 
As the expansion of the 
fledgling Aspect Shrines 
continued in the centuries 
after the Fall, it was inevitable 
that many of those who trod 
the Path of the Warrior had 
previously served as Guardian 
Officers – and would do so 
again upon leaving it.

Eldar seers were powerful 
opponents to anyone on the 
field, unleashing psychic 
blasts and seeming to change 
the laws of reality to bend to 
their will. Fearsome beyond 
doubt, rumours and rare 
pict-captures showed the 
deployment of entire units of 
seers bearing ancient weapons 
of distinct Eldar provenance - 
commonly called 'witch' 
weapons in low gothic - to 
battlezones throughout the 
galaxy.
These Warlocks, as they 
became known, displayed 
fighting styles akin to those of 
Aspect Warriors, rumoured to 
have walked both the Path of 
the Warrior and of the Seer, to 
devastating effect. Such living 
weapons were thankfully rare 
in the early days of the 
Craftworlds, and most 
frequently used as bodyguard 
units for valuable diviners 
known as Farseers. Their 
presence was a statement of 
the importance of a conflict to 
the Eldar, and a warning of 
powerful weapons taking 
shape not yet seen by Imperial 
Commanders at the end of 
the Great Crusade...

Those Eldar craftspeople who 
escaped the fall upon the 
Craftworlds found their 
talents in demand in the years 
that followed; for Aeldari 
technology had long relied 
upon the substance known as 
Wraithbone, and the 
Bonesingers were capable of 
literally singing it into shape, 
encouraging its growth into 
any shape and form they 
required. 
This skill was not without 
need on the battlefield, much 
to the sadness and reluctance 
of those who had taken the 
path of the shaper - for those 
wounds upon the battlefield 
both flesh and Wraithbone 
could be sung together, 
stitched by needles that 
threaded themselves, or hull-
plates bound back together 
with impossible speed as their 
craft healed under the Singer’s 
direction.

a creature of wanton 
destruction, laying waste to 
everything in its path in fire 
and flame. The Fire Dragon 
Aspect Shrine sought to 
embody this aspect of war, the 
all-consuming flame of their 
fusion weapons ripping 
through armor and 
fortification alike with the 
power of a star.
Heavily armoured and toting 
ferocious firepower, the Aspect 
Warriors of the Fire Dragons 
quickly gained a place on 
many Craftworlds reeling 
from the fall of the old empire 
due to the prolific journeys of 
their founder, Fuegan. 
Seemingly tireless, Fuegan 
was witnessed across the 
galaxy destroying the 
mightiest foes who would 
snuff out the last embers of 
the Eldar people. His students 
followed in his wake, showing 
that even after the fall, the 
light of the Eldar burned 
brightly into an Age of

## Page 42

Frequently overlooked, a 
diminutive scorpion's sting 
will cripple or kill predators 
hundreds of times their size. 
The heavily armoured aspect 
warriors of the Striking 
Scorpions sought to emulate 
these stealthy and lethal 
creatures, bringing brutality 
from the shadows. In addition 
to their potent chainsword 
and shuriken weaponry, they 
bore a pair of specialised 
weapon pods mounted to the 
sides of their helmets; these 
scorpion-stingers would 
project a hail of crystalline 
flechettes into a target at 
supersonic speeds, followed 
immediately by a powerful 
laser burst. This would 
instantly turn the attuned 
crystals into a plasma charge 
which would detonate already 
partially within the target 
resulting in a frequently lethal 
explosion.
Despite their effectiveness, 
some felt the Striking 
Scorpions were ill-omened, 
their founder Arhra having 
fallen to the dark lures of 
Chaos. In Arhra's place, a new 
Phoenix had risen to the 
mantle of leading the 
Scorpions; Karandras. Under 
Karandras' direction, his 
children would hunt Arhra for 
millennia, spreading the 
teachings of the Scorpion in

The Howling Banshees were 
so named for the banshees of 
Aeldari mythology; harbingers 
of woe and death whose cry 
was said to echo that of the 
Aeldari War-God, Khaela 
Mensha Khaine. As perhaps 
the most feared of all the 
Aspect Warriors, it is fitting 
they drew their inspiration 
from such horror. 
Swift and agile even by 
Aeldari norms, their acrobatic 
skill seeing them leap and 
bound over obstacles without 
breaking stride, quickly 
closing ranks with their prey 
to begin a bloody slaughter 
with all the untouchable 
grace and athleticism of their 
approach. What Howling 
Banshees lacked in sheer brute 
strength, they more than 
made up for with speed and 
precision. 
In addition to their panoply 
of blades, their infamous 
Banshee Masks acted as 
powerful sonic weapons, 
amplifying their battle 
screams to inflict severe 
damage on their foes’ nervous 
systems, even so far as causing 
temporary paralysis - leaving 
them helpless as sheer terror 
overwhelmed them. By the 
end of the Great Crusade, the 
war cries of the Banshees had 
heralded the end of many a 
foe - both Imperial and not...

Awoken only in times of great 
need, the Wraithblades were 
constructs that were kept 
apart from the other 
constructs of a Craftworld, 
lest the anger of the 
inhabitants of their Spirit 
Stones disturb others in the 
infinity circuit from their 
deserved rest. 
When woken by the 
Craftworld’s Wraith Guides, 
their rage was quick to spark, 
and they would set about the 
enemy of their Craftworld 
with weapons and shields 
sung from the same 
wraithbone as their construct 
bodies. 
Furious fighters upon the 
battlefield, the Wraithblades’ 
charge into the fray was unto 
the fury of Khaine himself. 
Few could stand against a full 
unit of Wraithblades in close 
combat, as their bodies flowed 
and move with an echo of the 
grace of their former lives.

The more calm and collected 
kin of the tempestuous 
Wraithblades, the 
Wraithguard made use of 
Wraithcannons and 
D-Scythes to cut their foes 
down from afar. 
Awoken only in times of 
greatest need, the act of 
awakening and committing a 
fallen warrior’s soul into a 
wraithbone construct was 
seen as akin to necromancy, 
immeasurably abhorrent in 
the eyes of the Aeldari. 
Despite these reservations, 
there are few who could deny 
the power these walking dead 
brought to bear upon the 
battlefield, as they waded 
through storms of fire towards 
their objective.
These venerable warriors were 
mostly from the time shortly 
after the Fall, with most of the 
earliest Wraithform shells 
having being taken from the 
armouries of the Anrathe and 
retrofitted to accept Spirit 
Stones. They are often led in 
battle by a Wraith Guide, to 
offset and account for the 
condition that afflicted many 
such wraiths, known as 
‘Wraithsight’. This dulling of 
the senses and slowing of their 
reactions in comparison to 
what these esteemed warriors 
had been able to achieve in 
life was deemed an acceptable 
side effect to being able to 
field an effective army to 
defend their host Craftworld.

## Page 46

While the anti-gravitic 
technology of the Eldar was 
well established, they were not 
without powerful walkers to 
journey into places that such 
vehicles were unsuited for. 
Most common of these was 
known simply as the War 
Walker, an avian-legged, 
bipedal strider that was both 
fast and powerfully armed. 
Protected by a potent energy 
shield, this scout was capable 
of tirelessly covering vast 
distances through dense 
terrain. 
While not as flexible as the 
Anrathe's Wasp Assault 
Walkers, the Craftworld's War 
Walkers were capable of being 
mass produced even in the 
smallest Craftworld's 
workshops, allowing even the 
limited the ability to bring 
heavy weapons to battle. 
While useful, their pilots had 
to keep mobility to the 
forefront of their minds as 
any weapon that pierced their 
energy screen was likely to do 
incredible damage to the 
machine.

## Page 50

With the already meagre 
population of the Craftworlds 
continuing to decline and the 
Aspect Shrines in their relative 
infancy, it became necessary 
for the Aeldari to raise militia 
levies to serve as the mainstay 
of their armies. It was a 
testament to the prowess of 
the Aeldari that, despite these 
militias being ostensibly 
composed of civilians, 
Guardians proved more than 
capable of standing against 
the Craftworlds myriad 
enemies time and again.
Guardian Defenders were the 
most common configuration 
of Guardian squad 
encountered, serving a 
battlefield role not dissimilar 
to the basic line infantry of 
humanity’s armies. Defenders 
acted in support of the more 
specialised, offensively-
focused Aspect Warriors and 
Storm Guardians, claiming 
ground and laying down 
withering hailstorms of fire. 
Guardian Defenders 
nonetheless appeared wary of 
allowing the enemy to close 
into melee with them, always 
ready to manoeuvre to 
another position and deny 
their enemy a chance to close 
into melee. 
In addition to their standard 
armament of Shuriken 
Catapults and Plasma 
Grenades, Guardian 
Defenders regularly crewed 
heavy weapon platforms, 
acting as mobile fire-bases 
that suppressed or outright 
destroyed many a foe.

Comparatively rarer than 
Defender squads, Storm 
Defenders were called upon 
when the grim calculus of war 
required ever-conservative 
Aeldari commanders to 
nonetheless commit dedicated 
shock troops to the fiercest 
sectors of the battle. 
Appropriately, many Storm 
Guardians were drawn from 
those civilian Aeldari with 
notable experience of war, but 
who were not otherwise 
serving as officers.
Storm Guardians forwent 
Shuriken Catapults in favour 
of pistols and close-combat 
weaponry, better suited to the 
bloody carnage of melee, 
though some were afforded 
more specialised armaments 
such as flamers and Fusion 
Blasters, apparently 
uncoupled from the rigidity of 
their Defender counterparts, 
perhaps befitting their history 
as warriors.

## Page 52

Those who fought the Eldar 
during the Great Crusade 
would often speak of their 
speed and powerful weapons. 
This was embodied at most in 
their Windriders, highly 
mobile jetbike formations 
mounting powerful shuriken 
or laser weaponry. Some 
Craftworlds fielded these as 
hereditary family units, or 
‘Windrider Clans’, and were 
capable of bringing hundreds 
or even thousands of such 
vehicles on to bear on the field 
at a time.
Only the small populations of 
post-fall craftworlds kept 
these formations from 
overwhelming Imperial 
outposts and agri-worlds 
unchallenged. Imperial 
commanders frequently had 
to call in aerial assets or 
Legiones Astartes forces to 
hunt down Eldar Windriders, 
so manoeuvrable were they.

The Dire Avengers were the 
first among the nascent 
Aspect Shrines, as well as the 
most common, for Asurmen 
founded more Shrines on 
more Craftworlds than any 
other Phoenix Lord. They 
were regarded as the 
protectors and the avengers of 
their Craftworlds; the 
representation of Khaine as a 
noble warrior. 
They were the least specialised 
of the Aspect Warriors, 
affording them greater 
tactical flexibility in their role 
as elite ranged infantry, often 
acting in support of Guardian 
squads at the heart of a 
conflict – rapidly shifting 
between offensive and defence 
tactics as necessary, for they 
possessed a seemingly 
preternatural ability to read 
the ebb and flow of battle.
While the Shuriken Catapult 
was a firearm synonymous 
with the Asuryani, the Dire 
Avengers were deemed the 
masters of its use – its 
wielding an art-form. 
Although numerous variants 
existed, the more 
sophisticated Avenger-pattern 
was unique to these Aspect 
Warriors. Although the Dire 
Avengers eschewed the 
carnage of melee, they were 
no less deadly for it, as all 
carried ceremonial knives as a 
weapon of last resort.

## Page 54

The foundation of the 
Asuryani is the Path. The path 
is not just a synonym for 
profession; far more than 
simply that, it embodies a 
series of restrictions and 
guides that work with the 
Eldar mind to contain its 
power and psychic abilities, 
channelling them into a 
productive and effective state.
Even in the early days of the 
Craftworlds, perhaps 
especially so, there were many 
who found this restrictive 
framework chaffing. With the 
empire fallen, the navy 
scattered, and the Exodites 
quietly isolating themselves, 
the Craftworlds became a 
major way-point for these 
roving wanderers in their 
travels throughout the galaxy. 
To protect themselves in their 
journeys, these Rangers 
frequently carried Long-Rifles, 
to stop threats before they 
were detected, and purchased 
as much with vital 
information as they did with 
relics of a bygone era brought 
to the last cradles of their 
people. In time most of these 
wayfarers would find a home 
and settle into a path of their 
choosing, but in the 
meantime, their welcomes 
had to be kept short so as to 
not draw the eye of the ever 
hungry malignity of She Who 
Thirsts.
52

The Wave Serpent was one of 
the more common forms of 
transport available to the 
Asuryani. Being both fast and 
capable of traversing the 
majority of gates in the 
webway network, the Wave 
Serpent traded much of the 
firepower seen in similar 
vehicles for approximately 
twice the transport bay. While 
this in and of itself was 
undoubtedly of great use to 
the early Craftworlds, the 
lower energy requirements 
also allowed it to mount a 
potent energy shield known as 
the Serpent Shield, and it was 
from this vehicle drew its 
name.
The Serpent was a guardian 
figure in Eldar mythology, as 
well as a deadly creature. The 
Serpent Shield embodies this 
duality, both reducing the 
impact of incoming fire and 
storing the kinetic energy of 
attacks to unleash in a 
terrifying wave known as a 
Serpent Strike. While this 
discharge frequently disabled 
the system for a short time, 
the effect was noted to pin 
entrenched forces to their 
fortifications or knock down 
those in the open field. This 
provided openings for the 
Eldar warriors inside to 
disembark and strike 
unopposed.

## Page 58

Of all the Aspect Shrines, the 
Shadow Spectres were most 
closely associated with the 
infamous Wraithguard. While 
the practice of animating 
wraith-constructs may have 
proven controversial among 
the Asuryani, it became 
tradition for all Shadow 
Spectres to continue their 
service beyond death, rather 
than enter their Craftworld’s 
Infinity Circuit.
There would be no release 
from war and duty, but until 
their deaths the Shadow 
Spectres still loomed across 
the battlefield like ghosts, 
appearing as pale, 
shimmering lights who only 
coalesced back into their 
material forms immediately 
before their powerful weapons 
were unleashed against a 
hapless target. In truth this 
seemingly supernatural 
phenomena was the result of 
the Spectres holo-fields, 
amplified by the 
manoeuvrability afforded to 
them by their jetpacks.
Equipped with powerful prism 
rifles, Shadow Spectres 
predominantly acted as tank-
hunters. These weapons used 
the same technology as larger 
prism cannons mounted upon 
Aeldari vehicles. Uniquely, 
each rifle was connected to a 
single targeting matrix called 
a Ghostlight, which allowed 
the Shadow Spectres to 
maximise the power of their 
rifles by converging their shots 
into a single, devastating 
beam of shrieking light. 
56

In Aeldari mythology wild 
hunting birds were associated 
with vengeance and 
retribution. It was believed 
that the spirit of a murdered 
Aeldari passed on to a hawk, 
who forever lingered above 
their killers as an inescapable 
mark of their guilt. It is from 
these legends that the 
Swooping Hawks derived 
their name; winged predators 
who soared above the 
battlefield to deliver death 
unto the enemies of the 
Aeldari.
Swooping Hawks were among 
the fastest and most agile of 
the Aspect Warriors, born 
aloft on specialised winged 
jump packs which enabled the 
Hawk to launch high into the 
air in the blink of an eye. 
With such speed did these 
wings vibrate that they 
became a blur of colour. To 
better enable this swiftness the 
Swooping Hawks wore 
thinner armour than other 
Aspects, typically in the 
colours of the skies through 
which they flew. 
Owing to their role as 
airborne harassers, Swooping 
Hawks were equipped with a 
variety of different grenade 
types, allowing them to 
engage in hit-and-run attacks 
against a range of targets 
while using their mobility to 
quickly take advantage of 
weaknesses in the enemy lines.

## Page 60

Among the rarest of the 
Aspect Shrines, the Shining 
Spears were the only Aspect 
Warriors who took to battle 
mounted on jetbikes. Serving 
as the spear of Khaine and 
their Craftworld, many 
indomitable foes were struck 
down by a single charge.
The Shining Spears were 
expert riders, able to complex 
aerial manoeuvres at high-
speeds, a testament not only 
to their skill, but also the 
exceptional calibre of their 
gleaming mounts, upon 
which they skimmed and 
weaved at breakneck speeds 
across even the most daunting 
of terrain. 
Most of the Shining Spears 
equipped Laser Lances; 
delivering powerful, short 
ranged blasts of energy on the 
charge. If the initial salvo 
proved insufficient, their speed 
enabled them to quickly 
reform or move on to other 
prey while slower elements of 
the Asuryani warhost finished 
off the weakened foe.

Within a Craftworld’s Infinity 
Circuit, invasive psychic 
entities were hunted down 
and destroyed by tiny, 
aggressive creatures called 
Warp Spiders. These 
arachnids would melt and 
crystallise their own bodies 
before reappearing elsewhere, 
affording the predators 
manoeuvrability akin to that 
of the Aspect Warriors who 
took their name for their own. 
More heavily armoured and 
thus ostensibly slower than 
most other Aspect Warriors, 
the Warp Spiders carried 
compact warp-generators 
which enabled them to make 
quick warp-jumps over short 
distances, disappearing and 
reappearing in the blink of an 
eye – Warp Spiders could 
engage in lightning fast 
attacks and vanish before the 
enemy was able to react. 
Despite the battlefield 
advantage these ‘flickerjump’ 
manoeuvres afforded them, 
they were nonetheless 
submerging themselves into 
the Warp and risking their 
very souls with every use of 
their generator. It was for this 
reason that the Warp Spiders 
were considered the bravest of 
all Aspect Warriors.

## Page 62

Many Rangers were able to 
find and secure relics from the 
old empire through their 
wandering of the galaxy. One 
of the more common of such 
relics was the Raptor Jetbike. 
Capable of rapidly moving a 
pair of Eldar through the 
webway or across planets with 
less maintenance and greater 
clearance than even the lithe 
Vyper chassis was capable of, 
the Raptor proved its use 
many times over in service to 
Rangers. While its small size 
and ease of maintenance was 
a great boon, the rare 
technologies required to 
fabricate them made them 
uneconomical for the early 
Craftworld forces, and as such 
these machines were almost 
entirely used by the roving 
bands that discovered them.
This rarity did nothing to 
make them less deadly. 
Capable of being both a scout 
and a sniper platform, the so-
called Shroud Runners were 
invaluable assets for an Eldar 
commander on any 
campaign. Indeed, more than 
Cohort of the Solar Auxilia 
witnessed their commander's 
head simply ceased to exist, 
followed by the hum of fastly 
retreating anti-grav motors.

The small and nimble patrol 
craft known as Vypers were 
similar to the Land Speeders 
of the Imperium in function, 
if not more efficient - and 
even capable of orbital drop 
and ascents. Piloted by two 
Eldar, the gunner was 
mounted in an 
omnidirectional turret, whilst 
the pilot was capable of 
keeping pace with even the 
fast moving Windrider hosts. 
Frequently used as scouts, or 
escorts for larger vehicles, the 
Vyper was an effective if 
fragile weapon for the 
coalescing war hosts of the 
early Craftworlders. 
Frequently outfitted with the 
deceptive Eldar Holo-Fields 
and advanced targeting 
arrays, there are apocryphal 
tales of these vehicles 
somehow phasing through 
walls or fortifications. While 
these stories are widespread, 
they are far from confirmed; 
such stories were often 
assumed to be nothing more 
than the realm of hysterics, 
for if it were true then surely 
the Imperium would have 
been unable to stand before 
the Eldar’s assault.

A powerful military fighter 
hailing originally from the 
armouries of the Anrathe, the 
Nightshade interceptor was 
both incredibly manoeuvrable 
and powerfully armed with 
potent laser weaponry. 
Capable of cracking even the 
void-shielded hull of a 
thunderhawk at speed, 
Nightshade interceptors were 
the purview of ace pilots and 
their destruction was prized 
by Imperial Navy 
commanders both during and 
after the Great Crusade.
During the dark days of the 
Horus Heresy, a new trend 
was seen amongst the 
Nightshades encountered by 
Imperial and Secessionist 
forces. For unknown reasons, 
across the forces of multiple 
Craftworlds, and across vast 
distances of space, 
pict-captures began to tell of 
red markings covering these 
nimble crafts. While it was 
impossible to tell the truth of 
the matter, it was theorised 
that this was some sort of ace 
marking, similar to those 
found on Imperial fighter 
craft. Certainly, their 
effectiveness in battle would 
indicate as much...

The incredibly manoeuvrable 
Nightwing Interceptor was 
the most common air 
superiority fighter of the 
Anrathe before and during the 
fall. Many such craft found 
their home upon the 
Craftworlds, for as a multi-
role craft capable of 
devastating hails of shuriken 
fire and pinpoint destruction 
from paired Bright Lances, 
the Nightwing was almost 
without equal. 
An incredible craft, its success 
lay in an adaptable wing 
design which allowed it to 
pull off pinpoint turns, 
slowing to a seeming stall in a 
moment, before turning and 
re-engaging with such speed 
that many Imperial Aces 
simply could not track them. 
Often seen escorting larger 
craft such as Phoenix 
Bombers and Vampires, the 
Nightwing was both the 
herald of impending raids and 
the warning of battle lines 
being drawn by the young 
Craftworlds.

Eldar Empire, the Phoenix 
Bomber had many variations, 
all of them carrying multiple 
and diverse weapon systems 
to rain death down upon the 
foes of the Eldar. The 
Craftworld version frequently 
housed both deadly shuriken 
weaponry, as well as a 
powerful Eldar Pulse Laser; a 
rapid firing and deadly 
weapon similar to an Imperial 
Lascannon. 
The Phoenix also carried a 
variety of deadly missile 
weapons with which to 
barrage targets, from armor 
piercing munitions to 
battlefield-control payloads 
that shrouded the area in 
darkness, or dropped 
plasmatic fire upon enemy 
positions. Rarely seen without

the enduring wrath of the

## Page 70

The ancient Aeldari Smith-
God Vaul was a craftsman 
without peer. He made 
wonders that he shared with 
the Gods and their children, 
helping them in all aspects of 
civilisation. Yet he was not 
without his dark side, and 
when the god of murder took 
his friends captive, he 
bargained a hundred peerless 
blades for their freedom. The 
last of these dread weapons, 
he took into battle against 
Khaine himself, and though 
he lost the fight, his strength 
in defiance echoed in the 
Eldar mind.
So were the support platforms 
of the Eldar named for his 
peerless capacity for 
manufacturing. Mighty 
weapons using powerful relic 
technologies garnered from 
millions of years of warfare, 
the Support Batteries bore 
weapons capable of launching 
monofilament to shred 
infantry formations, 
weaponising the very 
atmosphere to shake apart 
fortifications and tanks, or 
even sundering reality with 
the devastating distortion 
weaponry. This might made 
the Vaul's Wrath formations 
powerful area denial weapons 
on the field of battle. While 
few Eldar commanders 
wished to stand in a single 
position for long, when 
required to do so, the use of 
these powerful weapons was 
all but guaranteed.
68

The Eldar mythic cycles speak 
of a time before death; a time 
when reincarnation flowed 
their people from one body to 
another seamlessly and 
eternally. The Dark Reapers 
were the embodiment not of 
that ancient tradition, but of 
the new way of the world, a 
way where death's reach was 
both inescapable and 
permanent.
Masters of long-ranged and 
powerful missile weaponry, 
the Dark Reapers were a 
thankfully rare sight upon the 
battlefields of the Age of 
Darkness. Capable of ranging 
their opponents regardless of 
speed or terrain, these 
immobile and intimidating 
figures struck without pity. It 
is impossible to say why their 
shrine was so slow to spread 
given its notable effectiveness; 
though some theorise the 
spectre of death already laid 
all too heavily in the minds of 
the Craftworlds, and this in 
turn caused reluctance to 
accept these Aspect Warriors.

The Eldar Empire lasted for 
millions of years, and their 
mastery of many obscure 
sciences was built over this 
time to truly dominating 
status. Nowhere was this seen 
more clearly than in the ease 
and frequency that they used 
antigravitic technology. The 
Falcon was a hallmark of this 
power, being as common to 
its people as the ubiquitous 
and sturdy Rhino Transport 
to the Imperium.
With a flexible turret mount 
capable of carrying a 
multitude of weaponry and a 
reasonable transport bay in 
the main body, the Falcon 
was a workhorse of the early 
Craftworlds for its ability to 
both defend itself against 
anything it could not outrun, 
and to swiftly deliver its 
contents anywhere it might be 
needed. Many of these vehicles 
were equipped with 
additional thrusters allowing 
them to drop from orbital 
gates or waiting ships directly 
onto the planets that need 
their crucial supplies or 
reinforcements. 
70

As light permeates everywhere 
in the cosmos, so too did most 
species eventually weaponize 
it using laser technology. The 
Eldar of the Craftworlds did 
so with their expected 
potency, mounting massive 
focusing crystals on the turret 
of their Falcon chassis. This 
setup was known as the Fire 
Prism, and mounted 
additional batteries and 
generation systems to power 
the hungry demands of this 
truly awe-inspiring laser 
cannon.
Indeed, these focusing crystals 
were capable of directing far 
more energy than the Fire 
Prism was capable of creating 
itself. The earliest pilots were 
quick to take advantage of 
this, leveraging this and the 
advanced targeting systems 
of the chassis to fire directly 
into the crystals of other fire 
prisms, in effect combining 
their firepower into a larger 
and more terrible blast 
capable of damaging even the 
God-Machines of the 
Mechanicum. It was from this 
exceptionally dangerous and 
deadly technique that the Fire 
Prism drew its name, their 
crystals blazing on every facet.

ubiquitous Falcon Chassis, the 
Night Spinner was fitted with

monofilament weapon that

extruding and accelerating

The falcon chassis was a 
workhorse frontier vehicle in 
many ways similar to the 
Imperial Rhino Transport. 
Adaptable for many worlds, 
and capable of moving both 
Eldar and materials swiftly 
and securely to new places, it 
was modified in countless 
ways. One such modification 
seen more frequently near the 
time of the 30th millennium 
was the Firestorm. Trading 
out the flexible falcon turret 
for a powerful and long 
ranged ‘Firestorm’ Scatter 
Laser Array, these vehicles 
were capable of filling the sky 
with potent anti-aircraft fire 
to stop both alien raiders and 
aerial wildlife from 
approaching Eldar convoys 
on the ground.
While the Craftworlds would 
eventually phase these tanks 
out, regarding them as a 
second-line unit due to their 
phenomenal aircraft, in the 
days shortly after the fall the 
chaos of the webway and the 
shattering of supply lines 
meant that these tanks 
provided an important stop-
gap in their new military. 
Unlike the hyper-specialised 
Aspect Shrines, any on the 
path of the Guardian could 
operate these potent weapons, 
providing an invaluable shield 
to their fellows.

The Wraithlords were the 
grander kin of the smaller 
wraithguard and 
wraithblades, standing tall far 
above their still-living 
brethren. Only the most 
capable of the warrior dead 
could be summoned from the 
newly-created Infinity 
Circuits to be attached to 
these towering constructs. 
Such an act was only to be 
done in times of direst need, 
as disturbing the rest of the 
souls of the slain to rouse 
them to war once more was 
seen as nothing short of abject 
defilement of their well earned 
slumber. 
Many of these ancient 
constructs owed their origin 
to the armouries of the 
Anrathe, in the same manner 
as their smaller wraith-kin, 
the hallowed chassis they 
inhabited having fought since 
before the Fall of their ancient 
race. Some Craftworlds make 
greater use of these ancient 
warriors than others, with 
some using them in numbers 
unheard of on others, and 
reviled on many. 
Armed with a variety of heavy 
weaponry, a individual 
Wraithlord can be equipped 
for most any task, and 
whichever way they were 
armed, it could be assured 
than they would perform with 
utmost surety; whether 
splitting foes from afar with 
shuriken fire or beams from 
their bright lances, or cutting 
them apart in close combat 
with immense swords or 
bludgeoning them into the 
ground with their gauntlets.

A Wraithseer was a sight very 
rarely seen upon the 
battlefields of the Great 
Crusade or Heresy, as the 
precious souls entombed 
within them were guarded 
jealously by the kin that 
carried their legacy. In times 
of utmost need, an especially 
powerful Warlock could be 
called forth from the Infinity 
Circuit, and asked to stride to 
war once more.
Such a request was not made 
lightly, for if the wraithbone 
construct that housed such a 
venerable soul fell in battle, 
then the soul within risked 
being forever lost to the 
predations of She Who 
Thirsts. Despite this obvious 
and immense risk, however, 
such a construct was a 
terrifying force that could 
turn the tide of even the most 
desperate battle, and so this 
risk was deemed necessary on 
occasion.
The armaments of the 
Wraithseers were similar to 
the more common 
Wraithlords, making them a 
considerable threat at range; 
though the unique weapons 
and wargear the Wraithseers 
carried, in conjunction with 
their considerable psychic 
might, made them a deadly 
adversary. Indeed, this psychic 
might helped them overcome 
the 'Wraithsight' that blighted 
other wraiths, lending them a 
greater speed and acuity - and 
making it a deadly mistake to 
underestimate the speed and 
agility of a Wraithseer - and 
not one many make twice.

Wraithknights were towering 
constructs, eclipsing even the 
size and might of a 
Wraithlord with their sheer 
bulk. Many of these knights 
were taken from the ruins of 
Anarathe warships in the 
aftermath of the Fall, 
repurposed as guardians of 
their respective Craftworlds. 
Despite their size, they could 
run across the battlefield, 
their long-legged gait making 
them faster than even a 
charging Questoris Knight - 
whilst their array of ancient 
weaponry allowed them to 
unleash the fury of the Aeldari 
at a moment’s notice. 
Wraithknights provided a 
powerful middle ground 
position between the smaller 
Wraithlords and 
Wraithguards, and the much 
larger Titan sized constructs 
that stood sentinel over the 
largest and most important 
shrines of the Aeldari. 
The Wraithknights differed 
from their smaller kin with 
the fact that along with the 
soul of a slain Eldar, each 
Wraithknight was also piloted 
by an living Eldar - and at 
that, one which was their 
twin. Such a rare occurrence 
lead to a strong psychic link 
between the two pilots, to the 
point that the still-living twin 
was occasionally entombed 
within the cockpit 
permanently, bonding their 
souls and mind beyond death, 
and allowing the 
wraithknight to move with 
the speed and grace of a living 
Eldar.

Specialised for hunting the 
tunnels of the webway, the 
Skathach Wraithknights were 
a rare and terrible breed of 
Wraithknight. Piloted by a 
living Eldar separated 
permanently from their twin, 
they patrolled the endless 
tunnels of the Webway using 
esoteric weaponry similar in 
design to those of the Fire 
Dragon Aspect Warriors. 
These pilots spent most of 
their time communing with 
the soul of their wraithknight 
as they defended the webway 
from any that would trespass, 
bonding with it in a strange 
symbiosis. Rarely emerging 
into realspace, the rare 
occasions in which they were 
seen to appear were typically 
in defence of a webway portal 
under threat, or otherwise to 
aid an Eldar force upon the 
brink of defeat. Multiple 
hunting bands and small 
armies were been rescued by 
the sudden appearance of a 
Skathach Wraithknight after 
they had met with forces that 
grossly outnumbered and 
overpowered their own.
Along with their normal 
armament, the Skathach 
mounted Webway Shunt 
Generators, immensely 
powerful engines which 
allowed them to slip in and 
out of the webway seemingly 
with a ghost-like grace; 
keeping a solid location lock 
on them proving near 
impossible in a battle 
condition. This would often 
end only one way for their 
foes - for when they 
reappeared in realspace, 
cannons shining bright, they 
would lay a foe low before 
simply melting back into the 
webway to continue their 
patrols.

The Eldar Vampire chassis 
was already respected as a 
harbinger of oncoming 
assault elements in its Raider 
form, but rarer and far more 
feared was the deadly 
Vampire Hunter variant. 
Exchanging its transport 
capacity to support the 
systems required to mount 
titan-grade pulsars, the 
Vampire Hunter was capable 
of destroying the largest and 
most powerful machines of 
the Imperium. Indeed, its 
destruction was prioritised 
over all others by the agents of 
the Mechanicum for its ability 
to strike against their Titans, 
whose anti-air weaponry was 
far to slow to track the nimble 
craft during their attack runs.
Thankfully for the lesser races 
of the galaxy, after the fall, 
the number of Vampire 
Hunters still in use was 
exceptionally small. Building 
new Pulsars was a time-
consuming process that 
required specialist engineers 
and space that was at a 
premium on all but the largest 
of the nascent Craftworlds. As 
such, many Asuryani 
commanders only committed 
their Vampire Hunters to the 
skies as a last resort, or when 
air superiority was so 
thoroughly established as to 
be a foregone conclusion.

vehicles ever encountered by

weaponry to the battlefield on 
a manoeuvrable chassis, it 
spelled doom for the engines 
of the Craftworld's enemies, 
many of whom never saw the 
nimble vehicle who brought

## Page 92

The Avatar of Khaine was an 
incarnation of the Aeldari 
God of War, Kaela Mensha 
Khaine. After his apocalyptic 
duel with She Who Thirsts 
during the fall of the Eldar, 
Khaine's essence was 
scattered, and eventually 
coalesced within the 
Craftworlds which had 
managed to successfully 
escape their race's doom. 
The essence of Khaine was not 
quick to coalesce within the 
Craftworlds. Over the course 
of many decades, inside 
shrines, or hidden, deep 
places, an immense statue 
formed. Made of blood-red 
steel, cold to the touch and 
utterly still, the Avatar began 
its long wait. 
When the first Craftworlds 
discovered these statues, they 
understood on some primal 
level that these were the last 
fragments of their War-God; a 
final gift to them, but one 
that demanded a terrible 
sacrifice in return for its use...
90

TheHandofAsuryan,DireAvenger
Once known as Iliathin, the 
Phoenix Lord Asurmen rose to 
prominence only after the fall 
had wrought its evils upon the 
Eldar empire. Living upon a 
world bathed in the after-
effects of that great calamity, 
Iliathin contemplated his 
own death after being forced 
to kill his own daemon-
possessed brother - until he 
was rescued, unbeknownst to 
both him and his rescuer, by a 
child who had survived at the 
brink of death on the same 
tainted world. 
Eventually escaping the 
hellish expanse in which he 
and the child had dwelt, the 
pair travelled together, joined 
by others who were alike in 
mind, until eventually the 
group founded a shrine upon 
a barren world they chose to 
call Asur. This Shrine would 
become the first Aspect 
Shrine, and Iliathin took its 
name for himself, becoming 
Asurmen, first Phoenix Lord 
of the Dire Avengers.
Tutoring group which had 
founded the shrine with him, 
each in turn of his followers 
took upon a new Aspect of 
war - each understanding 
their new role as Phoenix 
Lords in turn. The child who 
had saved his life became Jain 
Zar, Phoenix Lord of the 
Howling Banshees - and as 
each other Phoenix began to 
spread their teachings to the 
Craftworlds, a glimmer of 
hope began to flicker in 
Asurmen's breast, that 
perhaps the light of the Eldar 
was not yet extinguished…

## Page 94

Baharroth – meaning “The 
Cry of the Wind” – was the 
first of the Swooping Hawks, 
the most vibrant and youthful 
of Asurmen’s disciples and a 
master of flight whose 
majestic presence on the 
battlefield was said to herald 
an inevitable Asyurani 
victory. 
Brother to Maugan Ra, 
Baharroth was the sun to his 
sibling’s moon, and was 
regarded as perhaps the 
fastest of all Aeldari, who 
struck with the force of a 
hurricane – easily evading the 
swings of his comparatively 
sluggish foes – before taking 
back to the skies in the blink 
of an eye.

TheTempestofStarlight,ShiningSpear
Drastanta was the Phoenix 
Lord of the Shining Spears, a 
truly dazzling sight upon the 
battlefield. Arrogant by 
nature but thoroughly 
ruthless, they were known for 
their harrying strikes, hitting 
hard before speeding off to 
avoid any retribution that 
was to be laid upon them. 
They had such a love of battle 
that they drag engagements 
out in order to enjoy the 
feeling of superiority they had 
over their foe.
All their speed and might, 
however, was an attempt to 
allay a deep and desperate 
fear in their soul; a repeated 
dream of Asurmen dead at 
their feet, a howing daemon 
of She Who Thirsts standing 
over his corpse. Drastanta 
vowed to prevent this 
outcome, and crafted a 
mighty lance beyond the other 
laser lances members of their 
Aspect Shrine possessed. This 
Celestial Lance could pour 
forth power in such quantities 
that it could literally shatter 
the spirit of its victims; 
erasing them from materium 
and immaterium both.

## Page 96

Founder of the Fire Dragon 
Aspect Shrine, Fuegan was 
utterly devoted to the Path of 
the Warrior in a way that 
even his fellow Phoenix Lords 
were not. In the pitiless 
destruction of the enemies of 
the Aeldari, Fuegan was 
witnessed to take grim pride; 
for in their utter decimation 
was the thread of fate forward 
for his race reinforced, and the 
skein tightened to secure the 
longevity of his people.
In battle, Fuegan's weaponry 
was entirely alike with his 
mentality - for in the searing 
beams he unleashed, or the 
immense blows he delivered, 
were his determination to see 
the Asuryani survive writ 
large.
Despite his brutal nature, 
however, his teachings and 
Aspect were reinforced with 
his nobility, his teachings 
instructing all who followed 
in his footsteps that no matter 
how righteous and true their 
actions may feel, they must be 
undertaken for the good of 
the future, and of the Eldar 
race - or be wasted forever.
94

TheShadeofTwilight,ShadowSpectre
Irillyth founded the Shrine of 
the Shadow Spectre on the 
Craftworld of Mymeara,
and his fate was evermore 
entwined with it. During his 
training as a disciple of 
Asurmen, Irillyth received a 
vision of Mymearan’s 
destruction. He resolved to set 
out and rediscover the 
Craftworld to prepare it for 
the wars to come.
Returning to Mymearan 
would not prove easy, 
however. Irillyth’s journey 
took decades as he explored 
many webway paths. During 
this time, the Phoenix Lord 
established numerous Shrines 
upon the Craftworlds he 
encountered before 
continuing his search. The 
Phoenix Lord’s efforts were 
not in vain, however, as in 
time he found Mymeara once 
more. Irillyth began to train 
its warriors in his own 
methods of war; stealth, speed 
and overwhelming firepower.

## Page 98

Karandras did not have the 
honour of being the first 
Exarch of the Striking 
Scorpion Aspect Shrine; for in 
truth, he was the second to 
take up the mantle, being the 
pupil of the first, Arhra. 
Arhra was not akin to the 
other Phoenix Lords, being far 
more merciless and cruel - 
alike with those Eldar who 
dwelt within the webway, who 
had taken to calling 
themselves Drukhari - and 
this darkness within his soul 
was to be his downfall. Driven 
from the Shrine of the Asur at 
the hands of Karandras, his 
name sundered forever, Arhra 
fled - and so Karandras set 
about rebuilding the Striking 
Scorpions into an Aspect 
worthy of respect.
In doing so, Karandras hoped 
to stave off the darkness that 
sought to claim both his soul, 
and that of his people; and as 
a consummate duellist and 
expert warrior, he was 
accepted as a true Phoenix 
Lord by those lords of the 
other Aspects in turn.
96

TheHarvesterofSouls,DarkReaper
Ever the furthest from the 
Asurmen’s vision of the 
Asurya, save Arhra himself, 
Maugan Ra acted with 
removed independence from 
the ideals of the other Phoenix 
Lords. Not for him was the 
flashing sword or the 
plunging spear. Rather, the 
Harvester of Souls would 
choose to execute his foes 
from great range, with 
unerring accuracy. These 
principles were fed into the 
creation of his legendary 
weapon, the Maugetar - a 
Shrieker Cannon with an 
inbuilt Executioner. The likes 
of this weapon would never be 
crafted again after the 
Phoenix Lord crippled the 
Bonesinger who crafted it for 
him, to ensure the weapon 
remained a singular example 
of its craft.
The Dark Reaper Aspect 
Shrine would be founded on 
the Harvester of Souls’ 
doctrine in war, applying 
impeccable precision to every 
shot fired from their mighty 
weaponry. Instructed by their 
Phoenix Lord, the Dark 
Reapers quickly became 
known as some of the most 
deadly ranged combatants 
among the Aeldari.

## Page 100

First among the revered 
warriors who became the 
Asurya and later one of the 
exalted Phoenix Lords, Jain 
Zar was famed for her furious 
speed and nigh-unmatched 
skill at arms. ‘The Storm of 
Silence’, as she was known, 
was a name earned a 
thousand times over as she 
proved her mastery in the art 
of battle and carved her own 
legend from the hewn flesh of 
those who stood in her path - 
accenting her victories in 
squalls of bloody rain.
It was Jain Zar herself who 
developed the Scream that 
Steals, and went on to 
instruct many in her arts - 
teaching the Aeldari to 
harness the anguish and 
despair that plagued them 
after the Fall and channel it 
into utter lethality. It was 
from these first lessons that 
the sword-maiden had spread 
across the stars that the 
Howling Banshees Aspect 
came to be, and to hear their 
blood-chilling wail was to 
know that the daughters of 
Jain Zar had come to claim 
the lives of those that dared 
stand before them.
98

WEBWAYGATE...............................................75POINTS
The foundation upon which 
the Eldar Empire was formed, 
and the main form of 
transportation for all its 
sundered remnants, was the 
webway. Capable of near 
instantaneous transportation 
from one place to another, the 
fall left the webway a 
dangerous but still-critical 
pathway through the galaxy 
for the survivors. Heavily 
traveled by rangers, and used 
to find vital supplies or deliver 
brave warriors to far flung 
battlefields, webwab portals 
were paramount to the 
continued survival of the 
Craftworlds.
Difficult to locate, hidden by 
ancient artifice as they often 
were, Webway portals were 
high priority targets for 
Imperial logisticians. Yet an 
assault upon one was not 
without its perils, as entire 
warhosts could appear, 
transported impossible 
distances, and likely guided 
with impeccable timing by 
distant farseers.

The Eldar are renowned for 
their cunning. Nowhere is this 
shown off as it is in the 
concealment of those 
locations that cannot be 
moved by vast holo-field 
arrays and augury jammers 
that make all but the most 
determined search simply 
fruitless. On the battlefield 
forward supply depots and 
necessary positions are often 
protected by Holo-Field 
Defence Lines, deployable 
equipment that allow the 
nimble Eldar to conceal their 
logistics from the prying eyes 
of their enemies, and muster 
in secret. 
Such arrays are potent, but 
not as powerful as the 
emplaced relics that preserve 
their temples and webway 
gates from prying eyes. As 
such determined commanders 
may find them, and reveal 
targets to strike. Of course the 
Eldar are aware of this, 
leading to their use as traps to 
ensnare the unwary.

## Page 116

With extreme rarity did the 
Troupes of the Rillietann 
descend into the battlefields of 
the Horus Heresy, but on 
those occasions a Troupe 
Master could ever be found at 
the forefront of the action, 
acting out the role of an 
ancient Aeldari hero on new a 
theatre of war. With every 
action they undertook, a 
Troupe Master would ever so 
slightly twist the strands of 
fate towards the 
unfathomable goals of the 
Laughing God.
Utilising the arcane arsenal at 
their disposal, a Troupe 
Master led the Laughing 
God’s servants in dances that 
drew their adversaries to their 
demise. Behind the whirlwind 
of flourishing blades, the 
grotesque features of a Troupe 
Master’s mask would 
constantly shift between 
nightmarish terror and 
dreadful, cackling mirth - 
mocking the inevitable demise 
of the one gazing into their 
maddening visage.
Disappearing as fast as they 
emerged, a Harlequin Troupe 
would leave only two signs as 
proof of their presence: the 
butchered corpses of their 
foes, and a single pennant 
bearing esoteric symbols and 
unfamiliar designs. It was 
deemed that this could only 
have served as a sort of calling 
card - proof that this bloody 
artistry was of their own 
making.

## Page 120

Where a Troupe Master 
played the part of the hero or 
the villain, taking centre stage 
in the Saedath with 
rambunctious panache, the 
enigmatic Shadowseers were 
cast in an altogether different 
role - though one that held no 
less import. For the 
performances of the 
Harlequins were ever 
intertwined with the 
machinations of destiny, and 
every role needed its player.
In battle, Shadowseers 
exploited their psychic 
abilities to unravel the 
structures within the minds of 
their foes and twist their 
perception to suit their own 
whims. The psychic influence 
of a Shadowseer could 
unleash manic hysteria in the 
ranks of the bravest warriors, 
implant false memories in the 
heads of their victims or 
outright break their psyche - 
driving them into irreversible 
madness.
Less understood was the 
Shadowseers’ intrinsic 
aptitude for divining the 
future. The relationship 
between destiny and the 
Shadowseers was altogether 
different to that of the 
Asuryani Farseers, who 
glimpsed the paths of fate and 
walked them to their own 
benefit. Shadowseers 
interpreted the future in the 
fulfilling of roles, as they stood 
witness to grand 
performances that were yet to 
unfold.

Exploding the head on an 
enemy commander in a spray 
of gore, terrifying those who 
were once so inspired by their 
presence. Detonating 
explosive munitions behind 
enemy lines to turn the very 
weapons of the foe against 
them. Picking off ever more 
desperate attempts at 
rescuing injured comrades 
until the enemy had suffered 
casualties many times more 
than the lives they could have 
saved. These tools were the 
strokes of grim comedy that 
made up the Death Jester’s 
trade.
Few in the galaxy were so 
adept in the art of terror, or 
took such delight in the 
creative tormenting of their 
adversaries. When faced with 
particularly entertaining 
victims, Death Jester 
occasionally paused their 
antics to laugh at their foes or 
offer a bout of mocking 
applause before engrossing 
themselves once more in their 
morbid work.
Masterfully wielding a variety 
of devastating heavy 
weaponry, a Death Jester’s 
commitment to revelling in 
the terrible comedy to be 
found on the battlefield can 
be matched only by the 
consummate skill with which 
they execute their grisly 
spectacle.

## Page 122

Perhaps the strangest of all of 
the Laughing God’s warriors 
were those mysterious few 
donning hooded coats devoid 
of the colourful checked 
patterning typical of the 
Rillietann. Among the many 
whispers surrounding the 
emergence of such a figure, it 
was one word that echoed 
more than any other - 
Arebennian.
Arebennian were duellists of 
unbelievable skill, capable of 
landing decapitating strikes 
before an enemy was even 
aware of the mortal peril they 
were in - and yet, unlike the 
other Saedathii, who might 
laugh with mirth at their 
enemy's idiot attempts to 
defend themselves, the 
Arebennian would remain 
silently impassive as they 
slaughtered their way through 
Astartes and mortals alike.
To the most psychically 
sensitive of the Aeldari it 
seemed that a terrible doom 
weighed heavy upon the souls 
of these warriors, tragic 
purpose and unspoken truths 
permeating every facet of 
their being. More unsettling 
however, was perhaps the 
mask an Arebennian donned, 
its curved horns and eerie 
elegance evoking imagery not 
beheld in the eyes of the 
Aeldari since the Fall.

## Page 126

In the centuries following the 
Fall, rumours would 
occasionally surface of sudden 
bursts of blurred colour and 
flashing swords carving 
elegant lines across the 
myriad battlefields of the 
galaxy. None remained long 
enough to reveal themselves, 
and indeed the majority of the 
Aeldari had written them off 
as nought but fanciful tales.
But one word ever danced in 
the unknowable void between 
shadow and myth, weaving a 
legend that would be millenia 
in the making. 
Rillietann. Harlequin.
Each tale of the appearance of 
the Harlequins told not only 
of their supernatural 
swordsmanship and their 
haunting laughter, but also of 
the curious masks they wore, 
constantly shifting to suit the 
unknowable whims of the 
individual wearing it. To gaze 
into the mask of one of the 
Rillietann in battle was to 
behold one’s greatest fears 
play out before them on their 
mind’s stage, and to allow but 
a singular moment of 
weakness in the face of such 
horror was to invite certain 
death.

Effortlessly soaring across the 
battlefield in a tempest of 
colour and light, the 
Starweaver was the primary 
means by which the Rillietann 
bore themselves into battle. 
The sheer velocity at which 
the Starweaver would travel 
was enough to confound all 
but the most arcane targeting 
systems, compensating for its 
poor armour with impossible 
speed.
To further protect its 
mysterious passengers, the 
Starweaver employed 
Holo-Field technology and 
Mirage Launcher salvos, to 
turn the craft into little more 
than a shimmering blur, 
disappearing as fast as it 
emerged before bringing 
devastating firepower to bear 
with its twin Shuriken 
Cannons.
The Starweaver took its name 
from the firstborn son of the 
Cosmic Serpent, who was a 
brave and steadfast ally to the 
Laughing God in ages long 
since lost. In those elder days, 
it was said that Starweaver 
would bear Cegorach upon 
his own back in times of great 
peril, and that each time the 
Laughing God was brought to 
safety. In the actions of the 
fabled warriors of the 
Harlequin Troupes, the echoes 
of these ancient tales were 
brought to life upon the field 
of battle once more.

In ancient Aeldari myth, 
Skyweaver was the third of 
the Cosmic Serpent’s 
offspring. As a being 
composed of hundreds of 
smaller serpents, each with 
their own desires and all 
speaking only in riddles, 
Skyweaver spread himself 
across the stars and told 
infinite different versions of 
Cegorach’s tales. Having 
spread his lies and sown 
confusion amongst the 
Laughing God’s enemies, 
Skyweaver would often lay 
these foes low with its fangs as 
soon as their backs were 
turned.
As echoes of these ancient 
tales, Harlequin Skyweavers 
integrated their ritual allegory 
into lightning-fast mounted 
combat - blending brilliant 
battle strategy and theatrical 
artistry into one and the same 
thing. Prismatic afterimages 
followed their movements as a 
brilliant cloak of scattered 
light, ensuring enemy firearms 
would find itself unable to 
land a shot as they plunged 
headlong into battle.
Unlike the jetbike mounts 
typical of their Asuryani or 
Drukhari cousins, Harlequin 
Skyweavers flew as a pair. The 
Skyborne Prince was tasked 
with the piloting of their craft, 
spitting hails of devastating 
firepower into enemy infantry 
formations from their 
Shuriken Cannon. Meanwhile 
the Great Falcon fought from 
the rear, laying armoured 
walkers low with their Star 
Bolas or cleaving the heads 
clean off of their foes with 
elegant Zephyrglaives.

Borne on silent anti-grav 
engines, Voidweavers acted as 
the ambush hunters of the 
Harlequins. Voidweavers 
employed polychromatic 
camouflage in order to 
manoeuvre into the perfect 
position before unleashing a 
devastating salvo of haywire 
or prismatic laser fire. Its 
heavy armament sacrificed 
none of its speed, allowing the 
Voidweaver to flee before fire 
can be brought to bear 
against it, leaving no trace 
beyond a mad cackle on the 
wind.
Named for the second of the 
Cosmic Serpent’s brood, the 
Voidweaver follows the role of 
its primordial namesake with 
ritual reverence. Ill-tempered 
and obsessed with displaying 
its superiority, Voidweaver 
refused to bear the Laughing 
God as his brother did. It did 
however, lend its strength to 
the role of Cegorach’s 
protector. Legends tell that 
Voidweaver grew so jealous 
and cautious that it grew a 
second head with which to 
look backwards so that it 
could never be caught by 
surprise.
As the Rillietann picked out 
roles from the oldest of 
Aeldari legends, so to did the 
Voidweaver similarly play out 
this role in the warmaking of 
the Harlequins, eerily 
mirroring the tales of ancient 
days in every facet of its 
design and strategy in the 
theatre of battle.

AspectShrine(X)
The budding Aspect Shrines were a facet of the paths of which the 
Aeldari walked, a discipline of which one dedicated themselves to - at 
the exclusion of all else. Each of these cults focused upon a different 
element of the Aeldari war god, honing themselves into a particular 
tactical role on the battlefield. Their minds and bodies were honed with 
endless practice until they became suffused with the aspect of Khaine 
those Aspect Warriors were intended to honour. When the Aeldari of 
the Aspect Shrines went to war, they did so as a group of specialists, 
practised to perfectly compliment one another’s roles on the battlefield. 
However, their numbers dwindled due to their newfound existence, and 
when forced to mass in numbers against some dire threat, they were 
forced to draw upon the greater Craftworld population in the hope of 
assembling a force capable of combating the enemy.

AsuryaniArmour
The Asuryani were not a dedicated military force - and the battlefields 
of the Great Crusade and Age of Darkness were unkind to those 
unprepared to fight upon them. As a result, rapid innovation was 
required by the Bonesingers of the Craftworlds, as well as raids on old 
Anrathe caches to bolster existing supplies. This would result in armour 
wholly unique to that strand of the Aeldari race, designed from the best 
they could muster to defend themselves.
MeshArmour
Fashioned from scales of psychically-sensitive armour in a fashion 
similar to chainmail, these suits were light, and preserved the mobility 
of those who wore them.
